14:T77a,## Summary

`persistMediaBytes` in `packages/agent/src/api/media-store.ts` (~400-406) writes `<sha256>.<ext>` with `fs.writeFileSync` directly to its final name and skips the write whenever a file with that name already exists. `writeFileSync` truncates and writes in chunks, so an `ENOSPC` or a crash mid-write leaves a truncated file under the correct content hash. Every later persist of the same bytes (a retry after freeing disk, the same image attached again, a backup restore) sees the file exists, skips the write, and returns a success URL that serves the truncated bytes with `Cache-Control: private, max-age=31536000, immutable`. Nothing repairs it: GC and eviction only consider mtime and references. The same pattern is in `writeStoredMediaFile` (restore path), `persistPrivateMediaBytes` and the pins ledger write.

## Reproduction (develop `a6863aa318`, real module in a temporary `ELIZA_STATE_DIR`)

```
pre-seeded 100-byte file under the sha256 of a 4096-byte payload
persistMediaBytes(payload) -> url=/api/media/<hash>.bin, file size on disk 100 (expected 4096)

on a 64 KiB tmpfs with a 200 KiB payload:
first persist -> ENOSPC, partial file exists (65536 bytes)
second persist -> success URL; route serves 200 with 65536 bytes and the immutable cache header
```

## Impact

One disk-full event permanently corrupts specific attachments in a user's history, and content addressing guarantees the corruption is reused for every future identical upload and cached forever by the browser.

## Fix direction

Write to a temporary sibling (`<name>.<pid>.<random>.tmp`), fsync, then rename over the final name, removing the temporary file on any failure (the auth package's account file writer already does this); treat an existing file whose size differs from the payload as absent and rewrite it. Apply to all four write sites and pin with a test that pre-seeds a short file under the correct hash.
15:T899,**Is your feature request related to a problem? Please describe.**

`ContextRegistry` seeds ~39 "LifeOps personal assistant" context definitions unconditionally at boot (`packages/core/src/runtime/default-contexts.ts`, registered via `_initializeCore` → `tryRegisterMany(DEFAULT_CONTEXT_DEFINITIONS)`, `runtime.ts`). Per-turn selection (`listAvailableContextsForRole` → `ContextRegistry.listAvailable`) filters only by role gate. For a character built around a narrower domain (e.g. a single-purpose trading/social-posting agent with no email/calendar/contacts/phone/health/screen_time/subscriptions/payments/tasks/goals/todos/game/browser/files/terminal/code plugin loaded), the full catalog still renders into the Stage-1 routing prompt every turn via `formatAvailableContextsForPrompt`/`{{availableContexts}}`, burning prompt tokens on categories the agent can never act on — for a 24/7 autonomous agent this compounds daily.

There's no supported extension point to trim it: `compose_state_providers` filters a different registry (provider names), and `pre_model`/`post_model` only see the already-rendered prompt string.

**Describe the solution you'd like**

An `unregister(id)` (or `unregisterMany`) method on `ContextRegistry`, or a constructor/character-level option to seed a reduced definition set (e.g. `Character.contexts?: { exclude?: AgentContext[] }` consulted in `listAvailableContextsForRole`), so a non-LifeOps deployment can drop irrelevant contexts without patching core.

**Describe alternatives you've considered**

- Regex-stripping the rendered catalog lines out of the prompt in a `pre_model` pipeline hook — works, but fragile (breaks silently if `formatAvailableContextsForPrompt`'s line format changes) and duplicates the id list outside the registry.
- Reaching into `ContextRegistry`'s private `#definitions` field — not viable, it's a genuine private class field.

**Additional context**

Confirmed still open on `develop` as of this writing — `ContextRegistry` (`packages/core/src/runtime/context-registry.ts`) is unchanged from our vendored pin (`184a4cc8`): `register`/`registerMany`/`tryRegister`/`tryRegisterMany`/`listAvailable` only, no removal path.16:T135f,Record model calls, context choices and tool results for each app-chat turn.

## Detailed Description

### Project / Coordination

- Project board: https://github.com/orgs/elizaOS/projects/15
- Parent tracker or epic: #17022
- Coordination Discussion: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/discussions/14292
- Suggested lane tag: `chat-trajectory-proof`

### Alpha Phone allocation

Owner: Nubs. September 11; one working day including the Alpha-relevant review/fixes below. Consolidates #30291 and #30734 without adding days or making batching a required deliverable.

- Reproduce the stats-width report on the current stats path. #30291 records a real database overflow at lifetime totals above 2^31-1; it is not evidence that current develop is still broken. Review existing #30292/#30461 before changing code. If still reproducible, preserve wide aggregate totals and verify the real endpoint beyond the boundary.
- Measure recorder overhead in a representative app-chat trajectory. #30734 reports repeated writes, but does not establish their contribution to end-to-end latency. Batch only if current measurements justify it; otherwise leave persistence unchanged and record that result.
- Any batching must preserve every record, ID, timestamp, parent/ordinal link, owner ordering, write barriers, capture/completion order, errors and flush/lifecycle behavior. Prove record equivalence with a real database and report transaction reduction separately from user-visible latency.

The required outcome is complete, usable chat trajectories. No standalone stats-hardening project or speculative persistence rewrite is added. Original reports retain their reproduction history.

### Scope

Make ordinary app-chat trajectories record the decisions needed to diagnose routing, context, and false-success failures.

The reported bad-turn trajectory showed `providerAccessCount:36` but `llmCallCount:0`, even though Stage 1 and the planner necessarily used configured models. The persisted trajectory therefore could not answer:

- what Stage 1 received and returned;
- which contexts/intents/candidate actions were selected;
- which model/provider handled Stage 1 and planning;
- which actions/tools were exposed, called, and returned;
- why VIEWS won over an owner routine;
- whether the final reply had a matching mutation receipt.

Scenario-runner file trajectories have richer stage data; bring the real app/database trajectory path up to the same diagnostic standard without leaking secrets or unbounded private context.

Record structured stages for message handling, retrieval, planning, tool/action calls and results, evaluation/final reply, and effect receipts. Include model/provider identity, latency/token usage, context/provider attribution hashes, candidate/retrieval scores, and redaction metadata. Errors in trajectory writing are diagnostics (`J7`): warn and `runtime.reportError`, never kill the user turn.

Likely areas:

- core message-service trajectory hooks
- agent/app trajectory database writer and API
- Trajectories app view/projection
- native JSONL/export/redaction tests
- scenario/app parity tests

### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] A fresh app-chat turn records nonzero model-call stages for Stage 1/planner/evaluator calls that actually occur.
- [ ] The trajectory shows selected contexts/intents/candidates, retrieval ranks, exposed tools, tool/action inputs/results, final reply, and matching effect receipt.
- [ ] Model/provider identity, timing, token/cost fields, cache metadata, and failure state are accurate.
- [ ] Provider context is attributed and bounded; secrets, credentials, and private content follow existing redaction/privacy rules.
- [ ] A trajectory write failure warns and reports an error under J7 without fabricating success or breaking the user response.
- [ ] The Trajectories view/API can inspect the exact workout turn end to end and distinguish VIEWS output from a routine mutation.
- [ ] App-chat, scenario-runner, and native JSONL schemas have tested semantic parity for the shared stages.
- [ ] Regression tests cover simple reply, multi-tool success, preview/no-save, tool failure, missing capability, and final receipt rejection.

### Evidence Plan

- [ ] Screenshots or recording: Trajectories view walking through the exact workout turn.
- [ ] Logs: recorder stage lifecycle, redaction counts, write failure/reportError path.
- [ ] Real-LLM trajectories: exact before/after transcript with visible Stage 1, planner, OWNER_ROUTINES, result, and final receipt.
- [ ] Domain artifacts: trajectory DB rows/API JSON plus native JSONL/privacy attestation.
- [ ] CI or local commands: core/agent/app trajectory tests, export/privacy tests, live scenario, app visual audit if the view changes, and `bun run verify`.

### Claim / Status Notes

Before working, comment `CLAIMING: app chat trajectory observability` with your lane tag, add this issue to Project 15, set `Status = Claimed`, and set `Claimed by`.

_Found during backlog triage._
18:T43d,Stock Android full chat turns can fail with MODEL_OUTPUT_INCOMPLETE after 256 generated tokens even when the caller requested no output limit. A warm installed-APK run reached this failure after about 115 seconds.

The Bionic branch in plugin-capacitor-bridge's makeGenerateHandler constructs maxTokens as lane.maxTokens ?? 256. The canonical local-inference BionicHostLoader preserves an omitted limit so the native host owns the model capacity. Inspection of the accepted APK's bundled JavaScript and the distinct error context identifies the mobile bridge path as the source of this injected default.

Remove the bridge-only default, retain explicit caller limits, and reject native incomplete-output receipts even when no explicit caller limit was supplied. Cover buffered and streaming framed transport, full prompt preservation, outputs beyond 256 tokens, explicit limits, and native exhaustion. Keep privacy and delivery deadlines unchanged. Device qualification must use a rebuilt installed artifact and the real full-turn API path; transport tests alone are not device proof.
19:T57f,The normal Android JNI staging task searches Bun/AOSP musl asset directories before using pre-staged NDK libraries. In the current cleanup acceptance run, following its missing-library instruction compiled a musl fused library successfully; Gradle assembled an APK, but the mandatory artifact audit rejected `__errno_location` as incompatible with Android bionic. Running the NDK staging tool produced valid bionic libraries. A subsequent build also exposed that the JNI CMake target requires x86_64 as well as arm64 while the staging task only fails early for missing arm64.

Scope: remove automatic JNI discovery in musl asset/cache directories; use explicitly configured library directories or NDK-staged jniLibs. Correct the generator and checked-in Gradle instructions to the existing stage-elizavoice-lib.mjs command. Require both supported JNI slices before the native build begins. Preserve Bun/AOSP musl assets and the separate compiler, explicit property/environment compatibility, cloud build behavior, and the final artifact audit.

Acceptance: with musl assets and valid bionic JNI slices both present, the default copy task preserves the bionic libraries; missing required slices fail with the correct staging command; normal APK artifact audit passes before installation. Installed local chat and persistence remain separate required acceptance. No toolchain or ABI audit bypass is proposed.
1a:Tc39,## Summary

Generated media that arrives as a `data:` URL (Google Imagen images, TTS audio, voice-first audio) is undeliverable on Discord and Telegram. Discord's SDK resolves the string as a filesystem path (ENOENT, caption lost with it); Telegram's `sendMedia` throws `File not found at path: data:image/png;base64,<whole blob>`, aborts the reply before the text is sent, and logs the full base64 payload twice.

## Where (develop `c2bb8860be`)

- Producers: `packages/agent/src/services/media-generation.ts:98-103` `bytesToAudioDataUrl` → `data:audio/mpeg;base64,…` (used at `:201` for TTS); `packages/core/src/features/advanced-capabilities/actions/generateMedia.ts:227` `data:image/png;base64,${result.imageBase64}` for base64-only image providers; `packages/core/src/services/message/delivery.ts:155` voice-first `data:audio/wav;base64,…`.
- The media-persist hook that would rewrite these into `/api/media/<sha>.<ext>` handles (`packages/agent/src/api/media-runtime.ts:127-151`, phase `outgoing_before_deliver`) runs from `processor.ts:713/1303/1500` and the autonomy execution facade; the action callback that delivers `GENERATE_MEDIA` output reaches the connector send handlers without passing through it.
- Discord: `plugins/plugin-discord/utils.ts:339-341` wraps the string in `AttachmentBuilder("data:…")`; for audio/video the guarded fetch rejects non-http and the catch at `:372-377` falls through to the same `AttachmentBuilder("data:…")`; discord.js 14.27.0 `DataResolver.resolveFile` then does `path.resolve` + `fs.stat` → ENOENT, and because files ride on the last text chunk (`utils.ts:690-700`) the caption in the same `channel.send` is lost too.
- Telegram: `plugins/plugin-telegram/src/messageManager.ts:1121-1160` sends attachments in a `Promise.all` before the text; `:1425-1428` throws `File not found at path: ${mediaPath}` with the whole data URL; `:1464-1476` `logger.error({ mediaPath, error: error.message })` logs the blob twice; the rethrow aborts `sendMessageInChunks`, and the turn's `delivery_started` marker (`:1575-1610`) refuses a replay.
- Slack: `plugins/plugin-slack/src/service.ts:2776-2793` rejects via the fetch guard and logs `{ url: media.url }`, i.e. the blob.

## Input → wrong output

`GENERATE_MEDIA` with the Google image provider (base64-only) or any TTS request → on Discord the caption and image are both lost (ENOENT); on Telegram nothing reaches the user, the log carries megabytes of base64, and the turn cannot be retried. Providers that return https URLs (OpenAI) work, which matches the live Discord evidence in PR #20460.

## Fix direction

Run the media-persist hook on action-callback deliveries too (so `data:` becomes a canonical media-store handle) and make Discord and Telegram resolve store handles to bytes; or decode `data:` URLs into a Buffer in `buildOutboundDiscordAttachment` and Telegram `sendMedia` (`{ source: buffer }` for Telegraf). Never put a `data:` payload into an error message or a log field (log a hash or length instead). Telegram should send text independently of attachments so one bad attachment cannot suppress the reply.
1b:T5ee,## Summary

The agent export bundle contains the agent's decrypted `settings.secrets`, and the import path writes a bundle's `settings.secrets` straight into the new agent, although the export is documented as stripping secrets.

## Where (develop `c2bb8860be`)

- `packages/agent/src/services/agent-export.ts:743-747` reads the agent through `db.getAgentsByIds`; `plugins/plugin-sql/src/base.ts:645-663` `mapAgentRow` runs `transformAgentSettings(agent, decryptedCharacter)`, so `agent.settings.secrets` is plaintext.
- `agent-export.ts:921-923` strips only `runtime.character.secrets` into `characterConfig`; `:932` places the full `agent` record in the payload untouched.
- `agent-export.ts:995-1002` on import strips `secrets` from `characterConfig` but spreads `payload.agent` (including `settings.secrets`) into `agentData` before `createAgents`.

## Input → wrong output

`POST /api/agent/export` → the decrypted JSON inside the `.eliza-agent` bundle carries every provider key under `agent.settings.secrets`; the only barrier is the bundle password. `POST /api/agent/import` with a bundle whose `agent.settings.secrets` was edited plants those values (keys, endpoints) into the newly created agent.

## Fix direction

Delete `agent.secrets` and `agent.settings.secrets` (and vault-shaped keys) in `extractAgentData` before building the payload, strip the same fields from `payload.agent` in `restoreAgentData`, and add a test that decrypts the produced bundle and asserts no `settings.secrets` is present.

Observed source: packages/ui/src/components/pages/ScheduledTaskEditor.tsx and ScheduledTaskEditor.test.tsx; plugins/plugin-scheduling/src/scheduled-task/runner.ts acknowledge implementation. The real fire API already exists.

1d:T792,A long-running owner-private calendar turn can finish its reads and then reject the authorized write solely because its ingress delivery-audience attestation expired after five minutes. The user receives no calendar event despite retaining the same owner, room and request.

Observed in core-flows scenario `live-note-to-calendar-stale-source`, run `29192f53-198d-4b05-a1bd-64d006500333`: two availability observations settled successfully; the follow-up retrieved the current note and then `CALENDAR_CREATE_EVENT` was rejected with `expired_attestation`. The scenario failed and no successful create receipt was returned. This was a simulated CLI-provider diagnostic, not release-qualified evidence.

The synchronous action gate rejects expiration before the existing async membership revalidation. Simply lengthening the TTL or ignoring expiration would weaken the existing contract and is not the intended fix.

Acceptance:
- An active, originally authorized turn can renew audience evidence from current trusted authority before tool exposure/execution and visible egress.
- Renewal is scoped to the exact runtime, turn, actor and destination, and ends on completion or cancellation; stale completed turns cannot acquire renewed access.
- Canonical-room renewal checks the current owner, room kind and complete participant census. Authenticated API renewal revalidates the host principal with fresh credential/session checks rather than cached ingress authorization.
- Revocation, changed ownership/membership/destination, unavailable verification, actor/runtime mismatch and cancelled work remain denied. Sensitive-disclosure bookkeeping survives renewal.
- Regressions advance the clock beyond the normal TTL without relaxing it; cover successful long work and each invalidation boundary. Re-run the note-to-calendar journey and inspect authoritative event state and receipts.

1f:T16bf,# Relates to

Action tasks loaded unrelated schemas, sometimes finished without requested readback, and rebuilt avoidable prompt context. Simple arithmetic could unnecessarily enter evaluation. Paused work could lose its original domain when the response handler returned a simple reply.

# Change

Keep the response handler focused on contexts and intents. Use task-scoped `DISCOVER_ACTIONS`, operation-specific schemas, canonical tool ordering, stable evaluator instructions and instruction-aware cache affinity. Document verification/reference codes no longer infer programming work. Complete authorized source text and receipts remain intact.

Continue against every requested outcome, with prerequisite barriers, verified receipts, explicit additional-budget resume, fresh authorization and cancellation. Saved schemas are not reused. Ambiguous effects are parked rather than replayed. Preserve external continuation-worker fixes. Completed simple answers stay on the one-call path.

Preserve exact FILE bytes and final-line-ending evidence. A scoped native object envelope addresses observed Cerebras Qwen argument boundary loss while retaining native schemas, validation, cancellation and canonical hooks. Other providers and handler/evaluator transport are unchanged. Distinguish document identifiers from integrity metadata in evaluation.

20:T1abc,Fixes and ports across the connector, search, MCP and wallet plugins. One commit per issue.

## #32027: outbound `data:` and media-store attachments (Discord, Telegram, Slack)
Fixes #32027. Ported from closed #32032 (credit @hermesagent270-commits).

- Adds `resolveOutboundAttachmentBytes` to core. It handles each kind of URL as follows:
  - `data:` URLs are decoded in process, with a size cap.
  - Canonical `/api/media/...` handles use the runtime's local fetch.
  - http(s) URLs go through the SSRF-guarded fetcher.
  - Filesystem paths and other schemes are rejected without reading disk.
- Telegram, Discord and Slack now upload the resolved bytes.
- Logs and delivery evidence carry only a scheme/host/path summary. The base64 payload no longer appears in the Telegram error.
- If a Telegram attachment fails, the text reply is still sent.
- Behavior change: Telegram no longer reads local file paths passed as attachment URLs. Treating a model-supplied path as a file on disk was the bug.

## #32026: bounded Discord attachment cache
Fixes #32026. Ported from #32032.

- The cache is now keyed by the stable attachment id instead of the signed CDN URL.
- It holds at most 64 entries (LRU) with a 2-minute TTL.
- Extracted transcript text is no longer kept in the cache.

## #31464: per-account Discord disconnect (partial, `Refs`)
`deleteAccount` used to be a no-op. It now works as follows:

1. **Persist a disabled policy first.** It goes in the runtime cache, keyed by account id and a SHA-256 fingerprint of the token. The token itself is never stored.
2. **Disconnect the account.** `DiscordService.disconnectAccount`:
   - blocks new messages for that account only;
   - drains its admitted turns, within the existing shutdown-drain timeout;
   - tears down its client, login retry, debouncer, managers and audio sinks.
3. **Siblings are unaffected.** Other accounts keep running, and the default alias moves to a remaining account.
4. **Failures are retryable.** A teardown failure throws before the manager deletes its row, and a retry repeats the teardown.
5. **Restart honors the policy.** After a restart, the service skips the disabled token and the provider stops listing it. Configuring a new token under the same id counts as a replacement and runs normally.

Remaining, outside this plugin:
- Removing the token from character settings or env. The host's settings owner has to do this; the plugin cannot write it.
- The issue's gating of all-channel onboarding completion, which belongs to the onboarding flow.

## #31789: typed keyless web-search failures (+ #31785 SSE binding)
Fixes #31789.

- `searchKeylessWeb` now returns `undefined` only when a search succeeds with zero hits.
- Every provider failure throws `KeylessWebSearchUnavailableError`, with:
  - a reason: `rate_limited`, `http_error`, `timeout`, `network`, `aborted`, `malformed_response`, `response_too_large`, `unsupported_content_type` or `provider_error`;
  - the HTTP status and `Retry-After`;
  - no query text or provider payload in the message.
- The search takes a caller `AbortSignal` and never dispatches after cancellation.
- The MCP response parser now handles SSE strictly (#31785):
  - it parses complete SSE events, including multi-line and unspaced `data:` fields;
  - it ignores notifications and responses to other request ids;
  - identical replays are collapsed, and conflicting or missing responses are rejected.
- Callers updated: the agent `WEB_SEARCH` action, the edge runner, coding-tools and the cloud keyless search. A provider outage is now reported as unavailable, not as "no results" / `no_match`.

## #29650: platform-filtered MCP tool discovery
Fixes #29650.

- `Tier2ToolIndex.search` now ranks the whole index, filters by platform, and only then applies offset and limit.
- It returns the total match count, and `SEARCH_ACTIONS` reports `totalMatches` and `hasMore`.

## #29930: EVM relative swap sizing (money path)
Fixes #29930. Reworked from conflicting #29933 (credit @agent-cortex).

- Half / max / percent are now sized from the balance of the token being sold:
  - **ERC-20:** the token's on-chain `balanceOf` and `decimals`. Symbols resolve through Li.Fi, the same way `SwapAction` does. `max` spends the full balance.
  - **Native asset:** the native balance, and `max` keeps the 10% gas reserve.
- All math uses bigint raw units, so large, dust and high-precision balances are never rounded through floats.
- A failed balance read, an unconfigured chain or a zero result throws `INVALID_PARAMS`. The code never falls back to another token's balance.

## #31110: Solana RPC failure shown as $0
Fixes #31110. Ported from conflicting #31127 (credit @hermesagent270-commits).

- The token-account and SOL balance reads now throw `ElizaError`:
  - `SOLANA_RPC_UNAVAILABLE` for an RPC failure;
  - `SOLANA_RPC_RATE_LIMITED` after 3 backed-off attempts on 429. The old code retried 429 without limit.
- `updateWalletData` no longer caches an empty portfolio when a read fails, so the last known value stays.
- A failed first refresh no longer makes the configured wallet look absent.
- The wallet routes answer 503 with the typed code.

## #30151: web wake word matched as a substring
Fixes #30151. Ported from closed #30152 (credit @agent-cortex).

- The trigger must now be a standalone token, so "elizabeth" no longer fires "eliza".
- The boundary rule is relaxed for scripts written without spaces (CJK, Thai and similar).

## #30228: canRespond reflects Cloud model availability
Fixes #30228

`CloudModelRegistryService` now evaluates the effective TEXT_SMALL/TEXT_LARGE ids against its cached, non-billable `/models` catalog. It returns a typed readiness: `available`, `unknown`, or `model_not_available` with `MODEL_NOT_AVAILABLE`, the config key and the model id.
- The read is synchronous and makes no inference call. A stale catalog schedules one bounded background refresh, with a 30-minute TTL and a 60-second retry after a failure.
- `computeCanRespond` returns false only when the catalog positively says a model is not listed **and** Eliza Cloud is the only registered provider for that model type. A catalog outage stays `unknown` and never gates chat.
- `/api/status`, `/api/health` and the WS status events now carry `modelReadiness`. The chat composer names the invalid setting instead of asking the owner to set up a provider.

## #16420: native iOS Cloud authentication (partial)
Refs #16420
- New `ElizaCloudAuthSessionPlugin` (App target, registered in `ElizaBridgeViewController`, added to the Xcode project). It runs `ASWebAuthenticationSession` with the claimed HTTPS callback `https://eliza.app/auth/callback` on iOS 17.4+. The login hosts are an allowlist, and the callback host and path are fixed natively. One session owns a sign-in at a time.
- `ios-cloud-auth.ts` reuses the shared mobile PKCE client (S256, config, token, ACK) with an in-memory verifier. It joins concurrent attempts and never replays a consumed code.
- The credential is written before the ACK into the Steward token store, which the storage bridge already backs with the Keychain.
- `useCloudState` takes the native path on iOS and keeps the device-code fallback when it is unavailable.

**Remaining (needs Xcode signing, a device, and a deploy):**
- Confirm the `eliza.app` AASA covers `webcredentials` and `/auth/callback` for the signed team and bundle.
- Enable the mobile app-auth config routes in staging and production.
- Make store builds Cloud-only by default.
- Clean-install device runs on iOS and Android.
- Decide on iOS 16.0–17.4. The deployment target is 16.0 and HTTPS callbacks need 17.4, so those devices keep the device-code fallback.

## #32016: agent export secrets (Fixes #32016)
The maintainer closed #32028 because a migration bundle is meant to carry credentials, and asked for a secrets-free export to be **opt-in** instead. So this PR does that:
- The default bundle still carries `secrets` / `settings.secrets`, protected by the bundle password. The module header now says so, where it used to imply they were excluded.
- `POST /api/agent/export` accepts `excludeSecrets: true` (core contract, route, UI client). That option strips both containers from the agent record and from the runtime character. A non-object `settings` gets a typed `AGENT_EXPORT_INVALID_SETTINGS` error.
- Import is unchanged. A default bundle restores the credentials it carries.
- Test: `test/agent-transfer-secrets.test.ts` runs on real PGlite. It checks that a default bundle round-trips `settings.secrets`, and that a credential-free bundle has no secret in the decrypted payload or on the imported agent.

## #31952: atomic content-addressed media writer (Fixes #31952)
Ported from #31973 and cut down as the maintainer asked:
- Persist, restore and the pin ledger now share one write-temp-then-rename helper.
- If a file already exists under a content hash but its size differs from the payload, it is rewritten.
- Dropped from the original PR: the extra fsync, the GC sweep and the logging.
- Test: `test/media-store-atomic-persistence.test.ts` runs on the real filesystem. It covers repair on persist, repair on restore, idempotent re-persist, and a failed rename that leaves no partial file. It fails 4/5 on develop and passes with the fix.
- Not done: the #30019 sub-item (the plugin-meetings duplicate writer) is outside this PR's scope.

## #31953: WebSocket fan-out backpressure (Fixes #31953)
Ported from #31974.
- A peer's queued bytes are the transport buffer plus frames waiting on host admission.
- A peer above the soft limit keeps getting every frame for a grace period. If it still hasn't drained, it is terminated. Above the hard limit it is terminated at once.
- Frames are never skipped on a live socket. The client sees the termination and replays from the event buffer when it reconnects.
- A ping/pong sweep prunes peers that stop answering.
- Note for review: #31974 was closed because of a general concern about caps. This version never drops frames silently; the only failure mode is an observable disconnect followed by replay.
- Test: `test/event-hub-backpressure.test.ts` uses a real `ws` server and clients. A stalled peer is bounded and terminated while a healthy peer gets all 2000 frames. A peer that stops answering pings is pruned.

## #31239: trade-mode write failure (Fixes #31239)
Ported from #31259.
- A failed `saveElizaConfig` restores the previous in-memory shape, including removing a `features` object or mode key the handler itself created.
- The route then answers a structured 500 instead of `ok: true`.
- Test: `test/trade-mode-persistence-http.test.ts` drives the handler over loopback HTTP with the real config writer, with the config path blocked by a directory.

## #31004: shared references treated as cycles (Refs #31004)
- `stringifyForDiagnostics` (core) and the JS runtime bridge marshaller now track only the open ancestor path. True cycles are still marked, and nothing is truncated.
- The scheduling DAG validation site was already fixed by #30932, and that PR has tests.
- Tests: `packages/core/src/runtime/json-output.diagnostics.test.ts` and `packages/agent/test/js-runtime-bridge-shared-refs.test.ts`, which runs the real host-node bridge.
- Tracker items outside this area are still open: `ui` tts-debug, `plugin-personal-assistant` redact-sensitive-data, and logger #25125.

## #31251: shared registries and ElizaError identity (Fixes #31251)
- `ElizaError` now carries a `Symbol.for` brand on its prototype and a `Symbol.hasInstance` that checks it. That makes `instanceof`, `isElizaError` and `toElizaError` pass-through work across bundled copies.
- Subclasses keep normal prototype `instanceof`, and look-alike plain objects are still rejected.
- The OCR registry (#31254) and the local-inference vision augmenter (#31257) were already fixed on develop.
- Test: `packages/core/src/errors.identity.test.ts` loads a second module instance and checks recognition in both directions.

## #31881: owner-private audience renewal during long turns (Fixes #31881)
The 5-minute TTL is unchanged. What's new:
- **Turn lease.** The message turn (plugin-assistant `turn-lifetime.ts`) opens a lease with `beginTrustedDeliveryAudienceTurn`. The lease is bound to the runtime, to this turn's evidence binding, and to its merged cancellation signal. It closes when processing settles.
- **Renewal.** Expired evidence is re-derived from current authority:
  - Canonical rooms re-read the room kind, the owner and the full participant census.
  - API evidence renews only through a host-supplied fresh principal check.
- **What renewal must match.** It succeeds only if actor, agent, room, destination kind, owner and census are all unchanged. Sensitive-disclosure bookkeeping carries over.
- **When renewal is refused.** After completion or cancellation, for a different runtime, and for evidence that was already expired when the turn began.
- **Where it runs.** Inside revalidation, before the planner's exposure gates, and before owner-gated tool execution.
- Test: `packages/core/src/security/trusted-delivery-audience.renewal.test.ts` (14 cases) advances the clock past the TTL. It covers a long turn succeeding and each invalidation case.
- Not done:
  - `chat-routes.ts` does not yet pass `revalidatePrincipal` to `attestAuthenticatedApiDeliveryAudience`, so authenticated-API turns still expire at 5 minutes. That needs a host-side fresh session or token check.
  - The `live-note-to-calendar-stale-source` scenario was not re-run (needs a live provider).

## #30825: actionable local backup size failures (Refs #30825)
- `POST /api/backups` maps `AgentSnapshotBudgetExceededError` to a 413. The response carries `code`, `stage`, `unit`, `observed`, `limit` and `retryable: false`, plus a message the settings dialog shows as-is. It exposes no paths or DB diagnostics.
- Unexpected failures still return the generic 500.
- The file-count refusal now has its own `file count` stage.
- Test: `test/backup-capture-http.test.ts` gains a case against the real HTTP server and PGlite. A sparse 200 MiB media file gets a 413 with no state-dir path in the body; once it is removed, the backup succeeds.
- Not done: streaming local backups through capture-v2, which is the L-sized part. Also no native-dialog capture.

## #29069: plugin-sql metadata filter (Fixes #29069)
`getMemories` now applies the same `metadata @> :filter::jsonb` predicate that `countMemories` already uses.
- **Why containment (#29074) and not per-key equality (#29735):** it is what `countMemories` already ships, the maintainer asked for both methods to stay on it, and switching count to equality would change existing callers.
- Test: `memory-metadata-filter.real.test.ts` runs on real PGlite. It covers scalar, multi-key, nested objects in any key order, no match, empty filter and absent filter, with get and count agreeing. It fails 5/7 on develop.

## #30881: complete authorized context (Fixes #30881)
- `recent-conversations` always inlines the complete authorized cross-room bodies. A recall action now only appends a room index.
- The provider no longer declares `overflowText`, so an estimated budget cannot swap the bodies for a body-free manifest.
- The planner half is already satisfied: `pipeline.ts` runs no dispatch-budget preflight.
- Test: `test/recent-conversations-complete-context.test.ts` runs on real PGlite with an attested owner DM. Bodies are inline with and without a recall action; before this change, the recall-action case had no bodies.

24:T1256,## Summary

The evaluator rendered the planner's base context verbatim. The room roster (`ENTITIES`), the platform user record (`PLATFORM_USER_CONTEXT`), the widget syntax catalog (`uiWidgets`), the cross-room manifests (`recent-conversations`, `relevant-conversations`), `CHANNEL_TOPICS` and the first-run notice reached every in-loop evaluation call although `evaluatorTemplate` never references any of them.

Live evidence:
- Audit 2026-09-13: the evaluator user message was byte-identical to the planner's; `ENTITIES` + `PLATFORM_USER_CONTEXT` alone add ~7K chars per evaluator call.
- Live 2026-09-14: ~7.6K chars of `uiWidgets` / `recent-conversations` / `relevant-conversations` / `CHANNEL_TOPICS` / `firstRun` on every in-loop evaluation call.
- Gate proofs: the in-loop evaluator user message omits People in the Room, `PLATFORM_USER_CONTEXT` and the widget catalog but still carries `RECENT_MESSAGES`, `FACTS` and `CURRENT_TIME`; calendar create/move/delete evaluator FINISH still selects `effectReceiptIds`; RESTORE_CONTEXT reads refresh the evaluator context.

What changes:
- `provider-state.ts`: `EVALUATOR_STAGE_PROVIDER_EXCLUSIONS` names the providers the evaluator stage never reads.
- `pipeline.ts`: after the planner composition, `runV5MessageRuntimeStage1` composes a second `ContextObject` from the same already-run planner state through the existing `extraProviderExclusions` mechanism (the one ambient turns use), copies the planner composition's metadata onto it (`providerDiscoveryEnabled`, `historyReferenceEncoding`, `loadedContextProviders`, the completion-context selection), appends the same planner decision event and hands it to `runPlannerLoop` as `evaluatorContext`. The loaded context catalog reaches it too, so a reference the planner loaded never reads as still pending at evaluation time.
- `planner-types.ts`: `PlannerTrajectory.evaluatorBaseContext?` and `PlannerLoopParams.evaluatorContext?`.
- `planner-loop.ts`: the trajectory stores the evaluator composition (with the post-tool reply instruction appended when that seed is present); a planner-side `RESTORE_CONTEXT` read refreshes it through `restoreEvaluatorProviders` alongside `modelBaseContext`.
- `runtime/evaluator.ts`: `renderEvaluatorModelInput` renders `evaluatorBaseContext ?? modelBaseContext ?? context`, so callers that supply no evaluator composition render exactly as before; an evaluator `contextRequest` re-run refreshes both contexts; `restoreEvaluatorProviders` carries the restored planner provider bodies onto the evaluator composition while keeping its exclusions, without a second composition.

Policy note: this is not a cap or truncation. Whole named providers are removed via the existing `extraProviderExclusions` mechanism, and the removal is limited to the evaluator stage: the planner prompt is untouched, `evaluatorTemplate` never references the excluded providers, and `RESTORE_CONTEXT` reads are honored on both contexts (planner read and evaluator `contextRequest` re-run).

Derived from stable commits 51d30cd5d9f, 82c59fa5506 and the merge-only repairs in a3cbd5e9e26; carved onto `develop` (8e9fcf42d98) with only these hunks.

25:Tf6d,Fixes #31957.

Anchor-relative schedules now resolve occurrences on adjacent local days. A wake observed at 21:07 plus 240 minutes fires at 01:07 the following day, even when that following day has no observation or static fallback. Due decisions and the next-fire index share the same occurrence resolution; persisted fires are consumed once and stale observations do not replay on later days.

The change preserves DST-aware local-time resolution, negative offsets, and missed fixed-instant calendar anchors. Fixed-instant handling requires every relevant probe to resolve to the same non-null instant; sparse observations cannot masquerade as fixed anchors. Missing or malformed observations remain unresolved.

26:T2204,# Relates to

Completion evaluation currently shares the response-handler model slot, so changing its model also changes unrelated stages. This adds an optional evaluator-only selection without changing default stage models.

# Change

`ELIZA_EVALUATOR_MODEL` requests a provider model for completion evaluation only. An explicit nonblank evaluator argument wins; per-agent settings precede the documented environment fallback. Blank/nonstring runtime settings preserve the existing slot. Selection, including absence, remains fixed during restoration and is resolved afresh for a new invocation.

Both message-pipeline evaluator paths forward the host's bound settings/model metadata. Response handling, planning, extraction and background model selections remain unchanged. A per-call override does not borrow context/output limits belonging to another registered model; unknown capacity remains diagnostic and complete input is preserved. Actual served-model metadata remains authoritative if an adapter ignores the request or falls back.

Default off: no profile or model configuration is changed by this PR. The pinned develop base already includes the merged restoration/settled-scope work. This PR's diff is evaluator-only selection and its supporting budget, facade, documentation and tests, plus the mechanical Health CI dependency described below; it does not duplicate those merged fixes.

# Evidence Details

The six actual message-pipeline fixture cases cover ordinary and settled-action paths with runtime, environment and unset selection. They assert that only the evaluator receives the override, metadata getters retain their receiver, and the domain read executes once. Restoration tests preserve original-source recovery and model selection through setting changes; a fresh invocation observes the new setting. HTTP fixture tests inspect the actual SDK request model and reasoning fields, preserving the other stages' selections.

The legacy fixture envelopes remain valid against the now-merged restoration contracts. The refreshed 47 assistant tests cover those contracts without importing any additional restoration behavior in this PR. 27:T2a6a,# Relates to

Fixes #31062 — `FinancesService.getUpcomingBills` compared bare `YYYY-MM-DD` due dates against the UTC calendar day, so a bill due today became `overdue` at 16:00 in Los Angeles and a bill due yesterday stayed `upcoming` until 09:00 in Tokyo. Supersedes the approach closed in #31064 per the maintainer review there: one time zone owner, the same resolved zone on both advertised surfaces, absent configuration distinguished from failed resolution, both entry points exercised.

# Risks

Low to medium; the change is confined to how "today" is derived for bill dueness.

- New `packages/shared/src/lifeops-normalize/calendar-time-zone.ts` is the single owner: `registerCalendarTimeZoneResolver(runtime, resolver)` stores one owner-zone resolver on the runtime (a global-symbol property, so a dist and a source copy of the module see the same registration), `resolveCalendarTimeZone(runtime, now)` resolves owner zone → agent `TIMEZONE` setting → host zone and reports the `source`, and `calendarDateKey(now, timeZone)` produces the owner-day key.
- Fail-closed: a resolver that throws is a `LifeOpsServiceError` 503 `CALENDAR_TIME_ZONE_UNAVAILABLE` (cause preserved); an invalid zone from the owner or the setting is 422 `CALENDAR_TIME_ZONE_INVALID`. No step falls through to another zone. A `null` owner zone is "not configured" and continues to the setting; that is the only fallthrough.
- `FinancesService.getUpcomingBills` derives `todayIso` from `calendarDateKey(now, (await this.resolveCalendarTimeZone(now)).timeZone)`; the wrapper rethrows the shared error as `FinancesServiceError` with the same status and code, which `runPaymentsHandler` already turns into `success:false` and `runFinancesRoute` into `ctx.error(res, message, status)`. The action constructs `new FinancesService(runtime)` and the route `new FinancesService(runtime, {ownerEntityId})` exactly as before; neither needs injection because the owner lives on the runtime.
- The personal-assistant plugin registers `resolveConfiguredOwnerTimeZone` in `init`, next to the other first-turn registries. It reads the owner facts through `ownerFactsToView`, so the active-travel destination override is honoured like every other owner-zone consumer, and it propagates a store read failure instead of substituting the host zone (the lenient `resolveOwnerTimeZone` used by reminders is unchanged).
- Behavioral change for operators: an invalid owner or `TIMEZONE` zone now surfaces as an error on the bills route and the dashboard action instead of a silently UTC-classified list. Documented in `plugins/plugin-finances/README.md`.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Moves calendar time zone ownership into `@elizaos/shared`, registers the personal-assistant owner facts as that owner, and makes the finances bill classification read the owner's calendar day from it on every surface.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking; additive exports).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes require a change to the project documentation, and it is included in this PR (`plugins/plugin-finances/README.md`, `TIMEZONE` row).

One bill due `2026-03-02`, `now = 2026-03-03T03:30Z`. Owner fact `America/Los_Angeles` → `upcoming` through both `runPaymentsHandler` (`dashboard`) and `GET /api/lifeops/money/bills`; owner fact `Asia/Tokyo` → `overdue` on both; owner fact `Mars/Phobos` → action `success:false` with `{status: 422, code: CALENDAR_TIME_ZONE_INVALID}` and the route 422 naming the zone. It also asserts the resolver registered by `init` resolves with `source: "owner"`.
- `plugins/plugin-finances/src/upcoming-bills-calendar-time-zone.test.ts` — stubbed repository; owner-zone, setting, host-default, precedence, 503 on resolver failure with cause, 422 on invalid owner or setting zone.

<summary>Domain artifact: owner facts read back mid-run before the scheduler tick was disabled</summary>

While iterating, the LifeOps scheduler tick's travel reconciliation saw the owner fact (`Asia/Tokyo`) diverge from the host zone (`UTC`) and opened a provisional travel record, which the resolver then honoured as the effective zone (`{"timeZone":"UTC","source":"owner"}`). That is the documented owner-facts behaviour, not a defect, so the test disables the tick and clears travel before each fixture:

facts = {

2b:T1751,A pause issued while a repeat task is finishing can be lost, or can restore stale bookkeeping and backoff fields. TaskService now writes pause/resume and execution bookkeeping through atomic key-level patches in the standard SQL and in-memory adapters. Explicit unsets remove lastError after success and baseInterval after an adaptive interval update.

Fixes #31764. Supersedes and strengthens #31773 against observed develop 626fb0c635273c. The original production fix is retained; the additional commit replaces an uncontrolled concurrent test with deterministic interleaving coverage.

Validation uses real TaskService, AgentRuntime and PGlite. Four scenarios hold a completed read while the other writer commits: pause-first and bookkeeping-first, each with successful or failed work. All four fail on the prior whole-write implementation and pass on the repaired implementation. They verify exact metadata, explicit unsets, failure backoff, a future due tick remaining paused, resume preserving bookkeeping and the next tick executing. Eight actual persisted row observations are included. Barriers affect only the created task ID and forward every database operation; no database mock substitutes for the mutation under test.

Risk: medium, because this adds an optional adapter method and runtime outcome contract. Adapters without the method retain a whole-object fallback, which is explicitly **not atomic**. The plugin-inmemorydb task patch tail does not serialize arbitrary updateTasks callers; this PR makes no all-writers serialization claim. Already-selected work may finish once after pause, as documented; pausing does not cancel a running worker.

No schema migration, UI, model prompt, provider or device behavior is changed. The additive contract is documented in source JSDoc. Existing caller behavior is preserved for unsupported third-party adapters, with the limitation above.

Current candidate: `2d88f2358ecffacf4c6aaa1507a44eeab29671de`. Full core passes 988 files / 13,345 tests (2 files / 3 tests skipped). Full SQL passes 31 files / 235 tests (1 file / 3 tests skipped). Earlier unchanged-source qualification passed all six owning type/lint checks, 10 real SQL task tests and 100 inmemory tests. Canonical root verification passed on this exact clean head: 373/373 workspace tasks, zero cached, all final audits (exit0, 5814.9s). Hosted checks remain pending until publication; no merge or deployment is claimed.

Preserved unsuccessful qualification attempts: the initial full core run failed only the obsolete trust-envelope assertion subsequently removed upstream by #31830; rebasing made the complete suite pass. The first full SQL run lacked its compiled package entry; the normal SQL build repaired that prerequisite. Its next full run timed out in the unchanged Electric reactive-query test at 10 seconds; that real file and the complete SQL suite then passed without changing the timeout or source.

Evidence: full baseline failure output, four passing race cases, complete observed task rows, test producer, source hashes and final-head command receipts are attached publicly below. Initial stdout-based observation produced no records because the harness suppresses console output; it is preserved separately and is not domain proof. File-backed test observation captured the actual eight rows without changing production code.

Evidence applicability: UI and native/device captures are N/A because this changes background task metadata storage with no rendered or platform-specific surface. Live model trajectories are N/A because task scheduling and concurrent metadata writes execute deterministically without model inference, action prompts or model output; real TaskService/PGlite state and worker execution are the acceptance artifacts.

## Remaining acceptance limits

2f:T1ffc,# Relates to

Fixes #31764 — `TaskService.pauseTask` / `resumeTask` and the post-execution persist in `executeTaskInternal` were whole-metadata read-modify-write cycles over the same JSONB blob, so a pause landing during a run either lost its flag (the task kept executing) or wiped the run's `updatedAt` / `failureCount` / backoff state.

Definition of Done: full standard in [`CONTRIBUTING.md`](../CONTRIBUTING.md).

- [x] This PR targets `develop` and is rebased onto the latest `origin/develop`
      with zero conflicts (`git fetch origin && git rebase origin/develop`).
- [x] `bun install` and `bun run verify` were run after sync, or any failure is
      recorded below with the exact unrelated blocker.
- [x] A reviewer can confirm the change works without reading the code, from the
      evidence attached below.

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased/merged onto the latest `origin/develop`; zero conflicts (`1eba34ba95`). Does not overlap #31729 (prepareStop cordon), which touches admission only.
- [x] `bun run verify` passes post-sync: 373/373 workspace tasks and all repository audits, exit 0 on `4542233921` with the pinned Bun 1.3.14 / Node 24.15.0 install.

# Risks

Medium (core contract + adapters), mitigated by an explicit fallback:

- New `TaskMetadataPatch { set, unset }` and optional `IDatabaseAdapter.patchTaskMetadata`. `IAgentRuntime.patchTaskMetadata` returns `"patched" | "missing" | "unsupported"` (the adapter member is excluded from the runtime's adapter-surface type). Adapters that do not implement it keep working: `TaskService.persistTaskMetadata` falls back to merging the change over the freshest snapshot and calling `updateTask`, exactly the previous behavior.
- plugin-sql implements the patch as one `UPDATE`: `(COALESCE(metadata,'{}'::jsonb) || $set::jsonb) - 'k1' - 'k2'`, so a concurrent whole-object writer cannot slip between a read and this write. `JSON.stringify` drops `undefined` values, which is why key removal is explicit via `unset` (`lastError` on success, `baseInterval` when a worker returns `nextInterval`) instead of the old `lastError: undefined` / `delete` on the snapshot.
- The core in-memory adapter and `plugin-inmemorydb` merge in place (the latter on its serialized task-mutation tail).
- Semantics of what the run writes are unchanged (same keys, same values); only the write shape changed from replace-all to patch. `dueAt`/`scheduledAt` handling in `updateTask` is untouched.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Makes both writers key-level so their keys commute. The pause flag and the run's bookkeeping are now independent columns of the same JSONB document rather than competing snapshots of it.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking; additive contract).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation (JSDoc on the new members documents the contract).

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

- `packages/core/src/services/task.pause-race.pglite.test.ts` — real `AgentRuntime` + `@elizaos/plugin-sql` on PGlite, real `TaskService` with a manual clock: `Promise.all([runTick, pauseTask])` on a due repeat task, then asserts the row has `paused: true` **and** the run's `updatedAt`/`failureCount`/`updateInterval`, that the next tick skips it, and that `resumeTask` clears only the flag.
- `packages/core/src/services/task.test.ts` — the fake runtime gains `patchTaskMetadata`; a new case pins the fallback path (`"unsupported"` → merged `updateTask`, `unset` keys removed) and one pins pause + bookkeeping coexistence.
- `plugins/plugin-sql/src/__tests__/integration/task.real.test.ts` — `patchTaskMetadata` sets and unsets keys in one statement, leaves the others intact, returns `false` for a missing row.

## Detailed testing steps

```bash
cd packages/core && node ../shared/scripts/generate-keywords.mjs && bunx vitest run src/services/task.test.ts src/services/task.pause-race.pglite.test.ts
bunx vitest run --config packages/scripts/vitest/real.config.ts plugins/plugin-sql/src/__tests__/integration/task.real.test.ts
```

Red on develop's `task.ts` (tests written first): the PGlite case fails with `expected { updatedAt: 1800000000000, …(2) } to match object { paused: true, …(3) }` (the pause was dropped) and the fallback case fails on `lastError` surviving. Green at this head: core task suites 44/44 (43 existing + PGlite race case; the two fake-runtime additions are inside the 43-file count), plugin-sql task suite 10/10, and neighbours `task-scheduler`, `runtime-stop`, `followUp`, in-memory adapter suites 65/65; `plugin-inmemorydb` 19 files, 100/100. Typecheck: core, plugin-sql and plugin-inmemorydb clean against a core dist built at this head; Biome clean on the 11 touched files.

38:T5a1,## Summary

Two egress-policy fixes on the reply-only recovery path:

- **Rejected stated time answered from the observation.** When egress rejects a reply because its stated date or clock time is ungrounded, the `CURRENT_TIME` provider's own rendering is the complete answer ("what time is it"), so it is returned directly instead of a second model pass that could invent a second date.
- **Bounded recovery evidence.** The recovery request carries the financial observation providers that ground a corrected quantity, plus `CURRENT_TIME` when a stated time was rejected, never the entire runtime provider store. On 2026-09-11 05:35Z about 380K characters of room history rode along on a `completed_side_effect` recovery and the rewrite exceeded the provider's context limit after the effect had already applied. This selects which providers are evidence; it does not truncate any provider's text.

Derived from `nubsstableDONOTDELETE` 588fe424eab and related batch commits (live-gated 2026-09-14; part of #31031, carved out as a standalone change).

# Relates to

Fixes #27591 — `/api/apps/relaunch` bypasses the OWNER/ADMIN role gate enforced
on `/api/apps/launch`. This PR closes the whole class: every privileged
app-manager mutation now shares the same gate.

Definition of Done: full standard in [`CONTRIBUTING.md`](../CONTRIBUTING.md).

- [x] This PR targets `develop` and is rebased onto the latest `origin/develop`
      with zero conflicts (`git fetch origin && git rebase origin/develop`).

      recorded below with the exact unrelated blocker.

      evidence attached below.

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased/merged onto the latest `origin/develop`; zero conflicts
      (`ebc808e3a67fb941e29153d89fc896524d32fe3c`).
- [ ] `bun run verify` passes post-sync, or the exact unrelated blocker is
      documented in **Known gaps / failures** below.

# Risks

Low. Adds the existing `canLaunchApps(actorRole)` check to privileged routes
before they read the body or touch the app manager. OWNER/ADMIN behavior is
unchanged. The gate is the same helper `/api/apps/launch` already uses.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

The agent's outer gate admits any authenticated host session — a non-owner host
session resolves to `actorRole: USER` (`server.ts:1811-1848`) — and
`resolveBoundaryRole` maps any non-`isAuthorized` caller to `GUEST`
(`server-helpers-auth.ts:470`). Only `/api/apps/launch` checked
`canLaunchApps(actorRole)`, so a non-owner could:

- `POST /api/apps/relaunch` — stop then launch (fixed in the first commit),
- `POST /api/apps/install` — install a plugin (code execution),
- `POST /api/apps/load-from-directory` — load apps from an arbitrary directory,
- `POST /api/apps/create` — invoke the APP create action,
- `POST /api/apps/stop` and `POST /api/apps/runs/:runId/stop` — stop apps,
- `PUT /api/apps/permissions/:slug` — change app permission grants.

All of these now return 403 unless the actor is OWNER/ADMIN. `GET` permissions
and viewer-only run routes (attach/detach/heartbeat) are unchanged.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking) — authorization.

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`plugins/plugin-app-manager/src/api/apps-routes.ts` — the gate on each
privileged branch.

## Detailed testing steps

```bash
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-app-manager test -- apps-routes
```

The suite (52 tests) now includes:

- A deny matrix over `undefined`, `null`, `"USER"`, `"GUEST"` for install,
  stop, run-scoped stop, load-from-directory, create, and `PUT` permissions:
  each returns 403 with its route-specific message and never calls
  `appManager.stop`/`launch`.
- Allow cases for OWNER/ADMIN on stop (app and run) and install.
- The existing malformed-encoding and relative-directory boundary tests still
  run (the latter now as OWNER).

Negative control (gates removed): 24 deny cases fail, confirming the tests
discriminate the guards.

3e:T121b,# Relates to

Fixes #31948.

# Summary

Invalid memory threshold settings could silently invert or disable advanced-memory behavior. `Number.parseFloat` and `Number.parseInt` accepted partial numbers and produced `NaN` for other present values; comparisons against `NaN` then defeated the 0.85 storage floor or prevented long-term extraction from running.

- Parse `MEMORY_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD` as a complete finite number in `[0, 1]`.
- Parse `MEMORY_EXTRACTION_THRESHOLD` as a complete positive safe integer.
- Warn and retain the documented defaults for invalid values.
- Defend both long-term storage paths with a finite confidence-floor helper.
- Cover invalid, adversarial, valid, `NaN`, and infinity cases.

The advanced-memory feature moved from `packages/core` to `plugins/plugin-assistant` after the prior head. This rebase relocates the independently approved fix to that current authoritative implementation; the defect still existed there on current `develop`.

# Risk

Low. Clean numeric settings retain their prior values. Invalid and partially numeric settings now produce a visible warning and preserve the existing `0.85` and `30` defaults. No public API, dependency, lockfile, prompt, model, UI, migration, or persistence schema changes.

# Sync and scope

- Base: `develop` at `51a69a9ae861c49e09216f2ed3e93af80df8f301`.
- Head: `6a247c307172077942f245acac50fb1fc7a2eaf7`.
- One rebased commit; exactly four advanced-memory source/test files change under `plugins/plugin-assistant`.
- The package scope in the commit and PR title is updated from the removed core location to `plugin-assistant`.
- The tests now read configuration through the public `getConfig()` method rather than reaching through a private-field cast.

# Validation

Current rebased head, repository-pinned Node 24.15.0 and Bun 1.4.2:

- Plugin-assistant focused threshold suites: 23/23 passed (2 files).
- Plugin-assistant typecheck: PASS.
- Biome over all four changed files: PASS.
- `git diff --check origin/develop...HEAD`: PASS.

Hosted workflow run `36253464299` passed on this exact head: Source checks in 31m19s, Billing payment replay E2E in 6m43s, Subscription authority PostgreSQL in 1m12s, and the aggregate All Tests Passed gate in 2s.

The prior head `3bf68597c6f791fe5920acbd3c297858f089bbcb` passed 23/23 focused tests in the former core location, core typecheck, Biome, hosted checks, and the stacked root verify gate (373/373 tasks). Independent review mutation checks killed each of three regressions: permissive float parsing, permissive integer parsing, and a non-finite confidence floor. Those results establish the patch behavior before the upstream feature relocation; current-head validation above covers the relocated implementation.

# Reviewer path

`memory-service.test.ts` initializes the real `MemoryService` with controlled runtime settings and reads its public configuration. `memory-items.confidence-floor.test.ts` exercises the production storage-floor helper. The production storage loop uses `extraction.confidence < minConfidence`; without the finite guard, a `NaN` threshold makes that comparison false and stores every extracted item.

40:T92e,## Problem

`loadDefaultMasterKeySync` resolves a single persistent process-wide master key, but it is invoked **once per credential file** decoded by `account-storage.ts` (`masterKey()` → `decodeAccountRecord`). A `listAccounts()` over N pooled accounts therefore re-resolves the same key N times:

- **Keychain hosts:** N `readKeychainKeySync` child-process spawns, each ~80-150ms (cold Node boot + `@napi-rs/keyring` dynamic load + Secret Service round trip).
- **Passphrase hosts:** N scrypt derivations at 2^15 cost, ~90ms each.

With ~17 pooled subscription accounts this is **~2.5s of serialized, redundant key resolution**, and it runs inside the account-pool **lease** that every chat turn takes before its first model call.

### Observed impact

On a live deployment the `model:TEXT_EMBEDDING` inference span measured **1.4–9.6s** while the embedding backend itself (`curl` to the same local endpoint) answered in **20-40ms**. `strace` of a turn showed the lease blocking on ~17 keychain child processes (fd read of the base64 key) back-to-back before the embedding HTTP call was even issued — the span wraps the lease, so the redundant key work is charged to the embedding.

## Fix

Memoize the resolved key per `service/account` for the process lifetime.

- The value already lives in process memory for the duration of every vault/credential op; the cache is keyed by the same `service/account` tuple the resolver reads.
- **Security posture unchanged:** no key is ever fabricated, failures are never cached (transient keychain/passphrase errors still retry on the next call), and the `ELIZA_VAULT_PASSPHRASE` length guard still runs before the cache lookup.
- Adds a test-only `resetLoadDefaultMasterKeySyncCache()`.

## Test

`packages/auth/test/master-key-sync-cache.test.ts` asserts reference identity across a 17-call fan-out (simulating a `listAccounts()` decode loop):

- **Fails on clean develop:** un-memoized resolver returns a fresh `Buffer` per call → `expect(key).toBe(first)` fails.
- **Passes with the memo:** one resolution serves the whole fan-out.

The test tolerates the absence of the reset helper (falls back to a no-op) so the *behavioral* identity assertion is the bidirectional signal, not the new API symbol.

Existing `keychain-process.test.ts` (7 tests) still passes.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
43:T1a90,# Relates to

Closes #31004 — original scope: shared-reference handling across diagnostic serializers and redactors.

# Change

`stringifyForDiagnostics` (`packages/core/src/runtime/json-output.ts`),
`serializeTtsDebugDetail` (`packages/ui/src/utils/tts-debug.ts`), and
`redactValue` (`plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/redact-sensitive-data.ts`)
each tracked **every object ever visited** in a single, never-pruned set. So any
value reachable from two places (a DAG — the same object/array referenced from
two keys) was serialized with the *second* reference replaced by `"[Circular]"`,
silently dropping data from recorded trajectories, evaluator diagnostics, TTS
debug lines, and redacted audit payloads. True cycles *were* collapsed and the
first reference rendered correctly, which is why this survived the first-byte
and integrity checks.

Fix: track only the **current path's ancestors**, not all visited nodes.

- The two `JSON.stringify` replacers prune an ancestor stack back to the visited
  key's holder (`this`) and test membership against that stack, mirroring the
  already-corrected pattern in
  `packages/ui/src/components/developer/trajectory-reader-data.ts:95-117`.
- `redactValue` now removes each node from `seen` after its subtree is walked
  (and returns the mapped array before deleting).

Material risk: none identified. The output for a *true* cycle is unchanged
(`"[Circular]"`), and a DAG now serializes to exactly what `JSON.stringify`
produces natively for a shared non-cyclic reference.

# Testing

Verified by importing the **real modified source modules** (Node type-stripping,
Node v22.23.1, no build step, no mocks) and exercising them directly.

Before (unmodified `upstream/develop` sources):

```text
redact DAG -> {"a":{"password":"[REDACTED]","label":"x"},"b":"[Circular]"}
tts DAG    -> [eliza][tts] probe2 {"a":{"id":1},"b":"[Circular]"}
json DAG   -> {"a":{"id":1},"b":"[Circular]"}
```

After (this branch):

```text
json DAG        -> {"a":{"id":1},"b":{"id":1}}
json arr DAG    -> {"x":[1,2,3],"y":[1,2,3]}
json cycle      -> {"name":"c","self":"[Circular]"}   # unchanged
redact DAG      -> {"a":{"password":"[REDACTED]","label":"x"},"b":{"password":"[REDACTED]","label":"x"}}
redact cycle    -> self === "[Circular]"              # unchanged
tts DAG         -> [eliza][tts] phase {"a":{"id":1},"b":{"id":1}}
```

Regression tests added (executed by the owning package suites):

- `packages/core/src/runtime/json-output.test.ts` (new)

Fixes #31891

## Summary

The scheduled-item editor labels its primary control **Run now** for manual starters but calls the `acknowledge` lifecycle verb. Acknowledging only changes state — the canonical runner never dispatches on acknowledge — so the owner presses "Run now" and **no run happens**. The UI test asserted the same wrong verb, so it kept passing while the feature was broken.

- `ScheduledTaskEditor` manual-starter primary button now calls `client.fireScheduledTask(taskId)` — the existing authenticated fire API (`POST /api/lifeops/scheduled-tasks/:id/fire`) that runs the task immediately through the same strict-fire path the scheduler tick uses. No new server surface; the API was already there, unused by this panel.
- Typed fire outcomes render visibly instead of being swallowed: `fired` succeeds; `raced` (another tick claimed the row — the run is happening) is treated as success without a misleading error; `skipped` / `dispatch_deferred` / `dispatch_failed` surface their `reason`/`error` in the panel's existing error line.
- **Acknowledging a delivered item remains its own operation**: non-manual tasks keep the `Acknowledge` label and still route `apply("acknowledge")`. The button's `onClick` branches on `isManual`, so both behaviors coexist.
- Test updates: the "Run now" test now asserts the **fire** call and explicitly asserts `applyScheduledTask` was NOT called (the old test pinned the bug); two new failure-sensitive tests cover visible `dispatch_failed` reporting (no `onApplied`, error text rendered) and `raced`-as-success; the verb-error test rejects `fireScheduledTask` since that is now the call "Run now" makes. `snooze`/`complete`/`dismiss` routing untouched.

# Risks

Low. The change is confined to one file (`plugins/plugin-native-agent/src/web.ts`), a Capacitor plugin **web fallback** used only when no native iOS/Android bridge is registered. It adds three `!res.ok` guards that mirror the pre-existing `chat()` guard; the success paths are unchanged (proven by regression tests). No public type changes — `AgentStatus` already declares `state: 'error'` and `error: string | null`.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

The Capacitor Agent web fallback (`AgentWeb`) did **not** check `res.ok` in `start()`, `getStatus()`, or `stop()`. The agent HTTP server deliberately answers lifecycle/status failures with core `writeJsonError`'s `{ error: message }` body and a 4xx/5xx status (`packages/agent/src/api/agent-lifecycle-routes.ts`):

- `POST /api/agent/start` → `503` "Agent runtime is not available and this server cannot boot one on demand" when the host cannot boot; or `500` with the reported state flipped to `"error"` on a failed boot.

Because the web fallback blindly parsed and returned the body, callers received a **malformed `AgentStatus`** missing the required `state`/`agentName`/`port`/`startedAt` fields:

- `start()` did `return data.status ?? data`, so on error it returned `{ error }` (no `state`).
- `getStatus()` and `stop()` did `return res.json()` directly, returning the raw `{ error }` body.

The server's designed `"error"` / not-bootable state was silently discarded, violating the repo DTO/error policy (a failed producer must yield an explicit error/unavailable state, not healthy-looking/partial data). This is an internal inconsistency: `chat()` in the same file already checks `res.ok` and throws.

**User-facing impact:** consumers cast the result to `AgentStatus` and read `.state`. `packages/app/src/main.tsx` `initializeAgent()` dispatches `AGENT_READY_EVENT` with the status, and `packages/ui/src/api/client-agent.ts` casts `Agent.start()`/`Agent.getStatus()` to `AgentStatus` and branches on `.state === "running"`. When a local-agent boot returns 503/500, the missing `.state` makes a failed boot surface as an unrecognizable status rather than the intended `error` state.

### Fix

In `web.ts`, added `res.ok` handling to the three methods (mirroring `chat()`):

- `start()` / `getStatus()`: when `!res.ok`, read the JSON body's `error` (falling back to `statusText`) and return a well-formed `{ state: 'error', agentName: null, port: null, startedAt: null, error: <message> }`.
- `stop()`: when `!res.ok`, return `{ ok: false }` instead of the raw error body.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation. The behavior now matches the documented `AgentStatus` contract in `src/definitions.ts`.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`plugins/plugin-native-agent/src/web.test.ts` — five added cases lock out the malformed-DTO regression, each asserting `.state`/`.ok` explicitly. Run:

```bash
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-native-agent test
```

## Detailed testing steps

1. **Before (fix reverted):** the four new error-path cases fail — `stop()` returns `{ error: 'stop failed' }` instead of `{ ok: false }`, and `start()`/`getStatus()` resolve without a `.state`.
2. **After (fix applied):** all 27 cases pass. `start()`/`getStatus()` on 503/500 resolve to `{ state: 'error', ..., error: <server message> }`; `stop()` on 500 resolves to `{ ok: false }`; a missing JSON `error` field falls back to `statusText`; and the 200 success paths still return the parsed status.

49:T81c,## Summary

The `NOTES` handler reads `text`, `note`, `title` and `query` as fallbacks for `content`, and `newText` as a fallback for `body`, but the action declared only `action`, `content` and `body`. Core's `validateToolArgs` rejects any undeclared key and `normalizeParamAliases` only renames keys a parameter claims, so every call using those spellings failed with `Unexpected argument` before the handler ran and nothing was saved.

This declares them where core expects them:

- `content.aliases = ["text", "note", "title", "query"]`
- `body.aliases = ["newText", "replacement"]`

The handler's fallbacks are unchanged. An alias sent together with an explicit canonical value is still rejected, per core's alias rule (never clobber a provided canonical value); the new test pins that too.

Closes #31114

## Evidence

Before (develop `cbf357b0bd8a`, real validator against the real action):

```
NOTES {"action":"create","text":"buy milk"} => {"valid":false,"errors":["Unexpected argument 'text'"]}
NOTES {"action":"list","query":"milk"} => {"valid":false,"errors":["Unexpected argument 'query'"]}
NOTES {"action":"update","content":"milk","newText":"oat milk"} => {"valid":false,"errors":["Unexpected argument 'newText'"]}
```

After: the new test drives the real `executePlannedToolCall` through the promoted `NOTES_CREATE` / `NOTES_LIST` / `NOTES_UPDATE` children and the `NOTES` umbrella with `text`, `query`, `title` + `newText`, and `note`; each call succeeds and the created/updated note carries the expected title and body.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Preserve UTC milliseconds, human local date/weekday/time/DST abbreviation, IANA zone, provenance and unknown-user policy in a single-line clock.
- Preserve world/channel names, channel type, participant count and every channel count in a shorter sentence; structured data is unchanged.
- Keep honesty, memory and answer-first rules in their existing authoritative system clauses; remove their duplicates from the default persona style. Shorten all seven shared style rules without removing their clauses.

A full-runtime greeting pair with identical user context and native schemas used **5,962 → 5,878 input tokens (84 fewer; 1.41%)**. Smaller paired persona/provider prompts saved 9–12%; that is not a whole-turn percentage. Cache hits differed, so no latency speedup is claimed.

## What kind of change is this?

Prompt representation improvement; no new context selection, truncation, schema or permission behavior.

# Documentation changes needed?

No public API or operational procedure changes. The attached review documents complete wording, clause retention, measurements and remaining opportunities.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`composeResponseState` now intentionally composes the minimal Stage-1 interpretation provider set, while `alwaysInResponseState` providers (including the owner-private `agreementPins` provider) are admitted for planning through `selectV5PlannerStateProviderNames`. The approved-pin case still asserted its obligations against Stage-1 state, so it failed once that boundary landed.

The case now composes the canonical planner state for every pin assertion, asserts Stage 1 does **not** carry the planner-only obligation, and retains every approved/proposed content, citation, chat/agent scope, unpinning and audience-boundary assertion.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (test alignment; non-breaking).

# Background

Without the `eliza-source` condition that subpath resolves to `dist`, and the plugin lane never builds `plugin-goals`, so the suite has failed with `Cannot find package` on every develop run while passing on checkouts that carry a built dist. 5e:T535,## Summary

`read_channel` (advanced-capabilities MESSAGE action) failed with `SOURCE_AMBIGUOUS` on the owner's API room when the only connector was Discord: the connector service registers a legacy source-level route beside one route per account, so a single account looks like two "discord" connectors, and a turn that names no account (the API room carries no connector envelope) had nothing to disambiguate. `soleConnectorFamily` resolves exactly that shape to the account-scoped route; distinct accounts, distinct sources, or duplicate routes remain ambiguous and still ask.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`plugins/plugin-elizacloud/AGENTS.md` (and its byte-identical `CLAUDE.md`)
documented:

```bash
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-elizacloud test:integration  # integration tests only
```

The package defines no `test:integration` script. Running the documented command
fails:

```
$ bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-elizacloud test:integration
error: Script not found "test:integration"
```

There is no integration lane in this package (`__tests__/` contains only
`unit/`; the manifest exposes `test`, `test:unit`, `test:e2e`, `test:live`).
The line is removed from both files so a contributor or agent following the
guide does not hit a failing command.

## What kind of change is this?

Documentation correction (no code change).

# Documentation changes needed?

This PR **is** the documentation change.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`plugins/plugin-elizacloud/AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, Commands section.

## Detailed testing steps

```bash
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-elizacloud test:integration   # before: Script not found
bun run check:agents-claude                                 # PASS: 157 pairs identical
```
